([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-accessibility))
- **dec-ai-native-patterns**: AI-native design engineering patterns — generative/adaptive UI, streaming as interaction, prompt-as-interface, human-in-the-loop / agentic UX, eval-driven development, designing for uncertainty, latency and cost as design constraints. Use when designing any AI/LLM-powered feature, building agentic or chat UX, handling streaming/probabilistic output, or planning for model error and cost. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-ai-native-patterns))
- **dec-approve**: Marks pending (⬜) or on-hold (🟡) rows in the decisions.md DEC table as PM-approved. Use /dec-approve {DEC-ID,...} for multiple IDs, or the --all-pending option for bulk processing. Supports --reject to reject and --hold to hold. ([weg-9000/Orange-pm](https://github.com/weg-9000/Orange-pm/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-approve))
- **dec-cognitive-load**: Cognitive Load Theory for interfaces — intrinsic (task difficulty), extraneous (poor-design overhead), and germane (useful mental-model building) load. Use when a screen feels overwhelming, deciding what to simplify vs preserve, chunking complex flows, or diagnosing whether friction comes from the task or the design. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-cognitive-load))
- **dec-core-principles**: Foundational design-engineering principles that govern where complexity lives and how interfaces stay fast and consistent — Tesler's Law, progressive disclosure, progressive enhancement, recognition over recall, optimistic UI, perceived performance, single source of truth, constraints as a feature. Use when deciding system architecture vs UI exposure, reducing user-facing complexity, choosing what to render/reveal, or justifying a constraint-driven system. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-core-principles))
- **dec-css-architecture**: CSS and styling architecture — scaling methodologies (BEM, ITCSS, CUBE CSS) and fluid/responsive technique (mobile-first, clamp() fluid type/space, container queries for component-level responsiveness). Use when structuring stylesheets at scale, choosing a CSS methodology, building responsive layouts, or deciding between media queries and container queries. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-css-architecture))
- **dec-design-system-depth**: Design system depth beyond "tokens are data" — three-tier token architecture (primitive → semantic → component), W3C DTCG format and tooling, scales as systems, component API design, headless/unstyled primitives, documentation as product, governance and versioning. Use when architecting a design system, structuring tokens, designing component APIs, choosing headless primitives, or setting up contribution/versioning. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-design-system-depth))
- **dec-discovery-validation**: Product discovery and validation frameworks — Jobs To Be Done, Continuous Discovery / Opportunity Solution Tree, Lean UX / Build-Measure-Learn, Design Sprint, Working Backwards, Story Mapping, Crazy 8s. Use when scoping what to build, validating an idea before committing, structuring user research, or turning a fuzzy outcome into testable solutions. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-discovery-validation))
- **dec-gestalt-principles**: Gestalt principles of grouping — proximity, similarity, common region, continuity, closure, figure/ground, common fate, prägnanz, uniform connectedness. The visual logic behind how the eye assembles elements into wholes. Use when laying out a screen, deciding what reads as related, designing cards/sections, or diagnosing why a layout feels disorganized. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-gestalt-principles))
- **dec-krug-laws**: Steve Krug's "Don't Make Me Think" laws — self-evidence as the goal, mindless clicks over fewer clicks, and ruthless word-cutting. Use when reviewing copy density, navigation clarity, or any flow that forces users to stop and think; a fast gut-check for cognitive friction. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-krug-laws))
- **dec-motion-animation**: Interface motion and animation — the 12 principles of animation applied to UI, easing and spring physics (ease-out entrances / ease-in exits), the FLIP technique for performant layout animation, and motion as feedback not decoration. Use when animating a transition, choosing easing/duration, building layout animations, or deciding whether motion is communicating or just decorating. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-motion-animation))
- **dec-nielsen-heuristics**: Jakob Nielsen's 10 usability heuristics — the bedrock checklist for evaluating any interface. Use when running a heuristic evaluation, reviewing a screen for usability problems, justifying a UX critique, or sanity-checking that status, error recovery, consistency, and undo are handled. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-nielsen-heuristics))
- **dec-norman-principles**: Don Norman's principles of interaction design — affordances & signifiers, mapping, feedback, constraints, conceptual model, and the gulfs of execution and evaluation. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-norman-principles))
- **dec-prioritization-frameworks**: Prioritization frameworks — RICE, Kano model, MoSCoW, Value vs Effort 2×2. ([jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon](https://github.com/jpoindexter/design-engineering-canon/tree/HEAD/skills/dec-prioritization-frameworks))
- **dec-product-metrics**: Product metrics and outcome frameworks — North Star Framework, Google HEART, AARRR (Pirate Metrics), OKRs. - **dec-quality-testing**: Frontend quality and testing strategy — visual regression testing (snapshot diffing) and the Testing Trophy (favor integration tests over the classic pyramid for UI). - **dec-rams-principles**: Dieter Rams' 10 principles of good design — the taste-and-restraint rubric (innovative, useful, aesthetic, understandable, unobtrusive, honest, long-lasting, thorough, environmentally friendly, as little design as possible). - **dec-rendering-architecture**: Frontend rendering and state architecture — CSR/SSR/SSG/ISR/streaming SSR/RSC chosen per route, state colocation and derived state, server-state vs client-state, Suspense and Error Boundaries as first-class. - **dec-software-principles**: Core software engineering principles for the engineering half of design engineering — SOLID, DRY/KISS/YAGNI, Conway's Law, Principle of Least Astonishment. - **dec-ux-laws**: The Laws of UX and cognitive heuristics beyond the common five — Doherty Threshold, Goal-Gradient, Zeigarnik, Peak-End, Serial Position, Von Restorff, Postel's Law, Parkinson's, Pareto, Occam's Razor, Flow, Banner Blindness. Use when justifying a UX decision with a named principle, designing progress/motivation, placing CTAs and nav, or setting response-time targets.
